## **THE INVITATION**

"Let's go to sleep, hmm? Up at 5 AM will come sooner than we think and it's been an emotional few days in all the best ways."

You smile, already moving toward the bedroom.

"Maybe, if you're really nice, we can keep the competition for first baby going to pass the time."

---

Morgan is on her feet before you finish the sentence.

"I'm *extremely* nice."

Kaylee is right behind her.

"I'm *nicer*."

"It's not about being nice, Kaylee. It's about *strategy*."

"Strategy? Morgan, this isn't volleyball."

"Everything is volleyball if you have the right mindset."

---

You laugh—genuine, warm, the kind of laugh that comes from watching two women you love argue about competitive conception tactics while following you to bed.

---

The bedroom is dim, the Austin evening filtering through gauze curtains. The king bed is massive, piled with white linens and too many pillows. The kind of bed designed for exactly this.

---

Morgan catches up first, her hand finding your arm, spinning you toward her.

"Three-day head start means nothing if I'm more *motivated*."

Kaylee appears at your other side.

"I've been motivated since the plane, Morgan."

"So have I."

"Then may the best woman win."

---

They're both smiling now.

Not at each other.

At you.

---

"Bed," you say simply. "Now."

---

They comply.

---

**[FADE TO BLACK]**

Your phone alarm cuts through the darkness.

Soft. Persistent. The tone you set specifically because it won't make you want to throw the device across the room.

Too warm.

There's a weight on your chest—Morgan, her face pressed into your shoulder, one arm draped across your torso, breathing slow and deep. Her hair is a mess, tangled across the pillow and your skin.

There's a weight on your other side—Kaylee, curled against your ribs, her leg thrown over yours, her glasses folded neatly on the nightstand. Her hand rests on your stomach, fingers curled loosely.

---

Neither of them is wearing anything.

Neither are you.

---

The sheets are a disaster. Pillows have migrated to the floor. At some point during the night, the decorative throw blanket ended up draped over the headboard like a surrender flag.

**TIME:** 7:52 AM MST

## **THE GREETING**

You open the door.

Three white missiles of pure joy launch themselves at you.

**Eisenhower** arrives first—the pack alpha, 95 pounds of muscle and dignity, his tail wagging so hard his entire body oscillates. He presses his massive head against your thigh, demanding attention.

**Zhukov** is next—leaner, faster, more vocal. He's making a sound somewhere between a whine and a howl, expressing what can only be interpreted as "WHERE HAVE YOU BEEN I THOUGHT YOU WERE DEAD."

**Rommel** hangs back slightly—the youngest, still learning pack dynamics, but his tail is a blur. When you crouch down, he breaks protocol and bowls into you, all puppy energy despite his size.

---

"I know. I know. I was gone forever. It was terrible. You're all very brave."

Zhukov licks your face.

Eisenhower huffs, as if to say *this is undignified but I'll allow it.*

Rommel has somehow gotten his entire head inside your jacket.

---

You move through the familiar routine.

Three bowls. Premium kibble. Fresh water. The dogs eat with military precision—Eisenhower first, then Zhukov, then Rommel. Pack order maintained.

## **THE COLLAR OF MANY VOICES**

You pull it from Spatial Storage—the artifact you acquired from Lyria's shop in Thornmar. Rare tier. Telepathic communication with bonded allies. Voice of the Wilds.

The leather is warm in your hands, inscribed with runes that pulse faintly when you touch them.

You settle onto the living room floor, cross-legged, the three Dogos arranging themselves around you in their natural formation—Eisenhower at your right, Zhukov at your left, Rommel in front.

---

## **CONNECTION**

You slip the collar on.

The world *shifts*.

---

The dogs don't speak in words. They never have. But through the collar, their consciousness touches yours—impressions, emotions, fragments of understanding.

**Eisenhower** radiates steady calm. Pack leader energy. *You-returned. Good. Pack-whole.*

**Zhukov** is sharper, more alert. *Gone-long. Worried. Strangers-came. (Nick. Shea.) Fed-us. Not-you.*

**Rommel** is pure emotion—excitement, relief, confusion. *ALPHA-HOME. PLAY? PLAY NOW? WHERE-WENT?*

---

## **TRANSMISSION**

You push back.

Not words—you've learned they don't process language the way humans do. Instead, you send *feelings*. *Concepts*. *Images*.

---

**LOVE.**

The feeling of warmth. Safety. The three of them curled against you on cold nights. Their weight against your legs. The trust in their eyes.

Eisenhower's tail thumps once. Acknowledgment.

---

**LOYALTY.**

The pack. The bond. The unbreakable chain between you and them. You would die for them. They would die for you. This is not negotiable.

Zhukov presses her head against your thigh. *Know-this. Always-know.*

---

**PROTECTION.**

Images of the house. The yard. The territory. *This is ours. Guard it. Protect it.*

All three heads rise slightly. Attentive. Understanding.

---

**HOME.**

The concept is complex—not just the building, but the people in it. Nick. Shea. And now...

---

## **THE COMPLICATED PART**

You push the next set of impressions carefully.

**TWO FEMALES.**

Images of Morgan. Images of Kaylee. Not prey. Not threat. Not pack—not yet. But *important*. Connected to you in ways the dogs need to understand.

Eisenhower's head tilts. *Different-females. Same-alpha?*

You send confirmation. *Yes. Both mine. Both ours, eventually. Both to protect.*

Zhukov's impression is curious, not hostile. *Two-mates. Unusual. But alpha-decides.*

Rommel is confused but accepting. *MORE-HUMANS-TO-LOVE?*

Essentially, yes.

---

**MATES. PARTNERS. FAMILY.**

You try to convey the human complexity—the societal expectation of one partner, the reality of your situation, the way both Morgan and Kaylee have chosen this. Chosen you. Chosen each other, in a way.

The dogs don't understand the societal part. They don't have a concept of "normal" human relationships. What they understand is:

*Alpha has two females.*
*Alpha is happy.*
*Pack is growing.*

That's enough for them.

---

**FEAR.**

This one is harder to send. You're not supposed to be afraid. You're the alpha. But you push it anyway—the vulnerability of what's coming. Meeting your mother. Meeting Kaylee's father. The weight of judgment.

Eisenhower presses closer. *Pack-supports. Always.*

Zhukov's impression is fierce. *We-fight-for-you.*

Rommel doesn't fully understand fear but offers what he has: *ROMMEL-HERE. ROMMEL-LOVES.*

---

**EXCITEMENT.**

The flip side. The joy of what you're building. The possibility of children—more pack members. The future taking shape.

All three tails are wagging now.

---

## **PLAY**

She already knows.

She moves first.

450 days to pretend.

Heavy.

---

**THOMAS HAYES**

The Lincoln is quiet.

The wipers have stopped—the rain reduced to mist.

Thomas doesn't answer immediately. His jaw tightens slightly. The knuckles on the steering wheel shift.

This isn't aerospace trivia anymore.

This is the question that keeps defense planners awake at night.

---

"You want the official answer or the honest one?"

---

"The honest one, sir."

---

He exhales slowly.

"The Nagorno-Karabakh conflict. 2020. Azerbaijan used Turkish Bayraktar TB2 drones to systematically dismantle Armenian armor, artillery, and air defense systems. Thousand-dollar munitions destroying million-dollar platforms."

A pause.

"Ukraine accelerated the timeline. FPV drones. Commercial quadcopters with grenades. Fifty-dollar solutions killing fifty-million-dollar tanks."

His voice is flat. Clinical. The tone of a man who spent his career building the machines that are becoming obsolete.

"The carrier battle group has been the centerpiece of American power projection since 1945. A Ford-class carrier costs thirteen billion dollars. Its air wing costs another ten billion. Its escort group—cruisers, destroyers, submarines—another fifteen to twenty billion."

He lets that number breathe.

"Forty billion dollars. Six thousand sailors. Floating within range of hypersonic missiles that cost two million each. Drone swarms that cost less than a rounding error on the carrier's maintenance budget."

---

**KAYLEE**

She's gone still. This isn't the father who talks about retirement plans and pot roast.

This is the man who spent three decades inside the machine.

---

**THOMAS**

"The battleship didn't become obsolete overnight, Mr. Lindale. It happened gradually, then suddenly. Pearl Harbor. Taranto. The men who built their careers on dreadnoughts couldn't accept that aircraft had changed everything."

His eyes find the mirror.

"We're in the 'gradually' phase now. The admirals know. The generals know. But there's too much money, too many careers, too much institutional momentum invested in the current paradigm."

A long pause.

"The 'suddenly' phase will come when someone loses a carrier battle group to a swarm attack. Not if. *When*. And it will be the most expensive lesson in military history."

---

Silence.

---

"You asked when. My honest assessment?"

He turns onto a tree-lined street. Bellevue. Expensive homes. Manicured lawns.

"Ten years. Maybe fifteen. The manned fighter has twenty years left, perhaps thirty if the pilot unions and defense contractors can delay the inevitable."

His voice drops.

"My generation built the most sophisticated weapons systems in human history. Your generation will watch them become museum pieces."

---

He pulls into a driveway.

A beautiful craftsman home. Two stories. Warm light in the windows.

A woman's silhouette in the doorway.

---

**THOMAS**

He puts the car in park.

Turns to look at you directly—not through the mirror. Over his shoulder.

"That's a question I've never been asked by someone your age, Mr. Lindale. Most young men who date my daughter want to talk about themselves."

A beat.

"You wanted to talk about the future."

---

He opens his door.

"Bring the bags. Linda is waiting."

---

**TIME:** 1:08 PM PST

**THOMAS**

He walks to the counter.

Pours himself a glass of water.

Takes a long drink.

Sets it down.

His voice is level. Measured. Giving nothing away.

**LINDA**

## **MECHANICAL**

---

"Gas system needs recalibration. Position 2 is too lean for sustained suppressed fire with the 500-grain loads. We either open up the gas port diameter by maybe fifteen-thousandths, or we add a fourth detent position specifically for heavy subsonics."

He taps his notepad.

"The detent walking issue from '09—I didn't see it today. Either I fixed it back then and forgot, or the spring tension has settled over fifteen years. Either way, I'm going to tear down the gas block tonight and measure everything. If the detent holds, we replicate it exactly. If it doesn't, we redesign with a positive-lock system."

---

He flips a page.

---

"Receiver and bolt are solid. No signs of stress cracking, no headspace issues. The milled receiver was the right call—she's overbuilt for the pressure curve, which is exactly what you want."

---

"Furniture—you said quad rail, standard configuration. Knight's and Benelli are handling the offset mounting for the Masterkey integration. I just need to provide a rail system that clears the 458 barrel profile and gives them something to work with."

He looks at you for confirmation.

"That still the plan? Standard quad rail, no offset work on my end?"

---

"That's the plan. Let them handle the integration. You focus on making a thousand rifles that run like this one just did."

---

"Copy."

---

"You've already handled most of this."

He gestures vaguely at his phone.

"We gained twelve thousand followers in the last hour. The video of you running failure drills suppressed is going to break the internet when we post it. Knight's, Benelli, and us are all playing along publicly."

He scratches his beard.

---

He looks at you.

"How do you want to structure that? Pre-orders? Waitlist? Limited drops?"

---

"And what's the MSRP? You're paying $3,200 per unit at cost. If I'm selling retail, I need to cover overhead, margin, warranty, customer service..."

He does the math in his head.

"Retail probably needs to be $4,500 to $5,200 depending on configuration. Suppressor-ready, milled receiver, adjustable gas, quality furniture—that's not unreasonable for what this is. But it's also not cheap."

"Tag you in the videos. Let you handle promo. Feed you content for the big channels and influencers."

He nods slowly.

"I can do that. Tromix has never been a marketing company—we're machinists who happen to sell things. But if you're willing to be the face..."

---

He trails off.

---

"Four thousand units. You really think we can move four thousand?"

---

"I think if KAC, Benelli, Staccato, and I are all pointing at you by March, you're going to have more demand than you can handle. The question isn't whether you can sell four thousand. The question is whether you can *build* four thousand."

---

Tony's quiet for a moment.

---

"That's... that's a different scale than I've ever operated at."

---

"Then scale."

---

## **ON NAMING**

---

"The Arbiter," he repeats. "Staccato collab pistol."

He chews on that.

"Something that integrates. Something that sounds like it belongs in the same family."

---

He's quiet for a long moment.

---

"The Adjudicator."

---

He says it slowly. Testing it.

---

"The Arbiter decides. The Adjudicator *enforces*."

---

A pause.

---

"Or we go simpler. The Verdict. The Ruling. Something in that legal-judgment family."

---

He shrugs.

---

"But you're right—that's sizzle, not steak. We can finalize naming later. What matters is that she runs, she's modern, and she's ready when you need her."

---

## **ON SALES CHANNELS**

---

"You're right that this isn't a Bass Pro wall-hanger. This is a specialty product. Direct sales. Maybe a handful of high-end dealers who understand what they're selling."

---

He taps the tablet.

---

"Waitlist model, probably. Deposits to confirm orders. Production batches. No inventory sitting on shelves—everything built to order."

---

"That keeps overhead low, maintains exclusivity, and lets us scale production based on actual demand rather than guessing."

---

He looks at you.

---

"I'll talk to Debbie about setting up the infrastructure. Website refresh, deposit system, CRM for the waitlist. We've got the bones—we just need to modernize."

---

---

Tony picks up the rifle.

Holds it carefully.

"She's yours until the first production unit rolls off. Bring her to the influencer shoots. Bring her to the news interviews. Let people see what she can do."

He hands it to you.
